NeST: Neuron Selective Tuning for LLM Safety
Authors: Sasha Behrouzi, Lichao Wu, Mohamadreza Rostami, Ahmad-Reza Sadeghi
First: 2026-02-18T20:01:01+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-12T11:06:43+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment is essential for the responsible deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs). Yet, existing approaches often rely on heavyweight fine-tuning that is costly to update, audit, and maintain across model families. Full fine-tuning incurs substantial computational and storage overhead, while parameter-efficient methods, e.g., Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), trade efficiency for inconsistent safety gains and sensitivity to design choices. Safety intervention mechanisms reduce unsafe outputs without modifying model weights, but do not directly shape or preserve the internal representations that govern safety behavior. We present NeST, a Neuron-Selective Tuning framework for efficient post-hoc safety alignment. NeST identifies safety-relevant feed-forward neurons via activation probing on vanilla harmful and benign prompts, clusters neurons with similar activation profiles, and trains shared cluster-level updates while freezing the rest of the model. Importantly, NeST is trained only on vanilla malicious prompts, without using jailbreak-specific attack data, yet generalizes robustly to diverse jailbreaks. The learned updates are then folded into the original weights, incurring no inference-time overhead. Evaluated on 14 open-weight language and multimodal models, NeST outperforms lightweight baselines and approaches full fine-tuning robustness with significantly fewer trainable parameters. On text-only models, NeST reduces average jailbreak attack success rate from 44.5% to 1.1% while training only 0.4M parameters on average. Across multimodal settings, it reduces ASR from 55.3% to 1.1%, and for downstream fine-tuned variants, it restores safety by reducing ASR from 53.8% to 0.8%. These results show that robust, maintainable safety alignment can be achieved by concentrating adaptation on localized, functionally coherent safety structures.
中文标题/摘要
标题：NeST：用于大型语言模型安全的神经元选择性调优
安全对齐对于大型语言模型（LLMs）的负责任部署至关重要。然而，现有方法通常依赖于成本高昂的重调优，难以在模型家族中进行更新、审计和维护。完全调优会产生大量的计算和存储开销，而参数高效的方法，如低秩适应（LoRA），则在效率与安全收益不一致和对设计选择敏感之间进行权衡。安全干预机制在不修改模型权重的情况下减少不安全输出，但并未直接塑造或保留影响安全行为的内部表示。 我们提出了NeST，一种用于高效事后安全对齐的神经元选择性调优框架。NeST通过对普通有害和良性提示的激活探测，识别与安全相关的前馈神经元，聚类具有相似激活特征的神经元，并在冻结模型其余部分的同时训练共享的聚类级更新。重要的是，NeST仅在普通恶意提示上进行训练，而不使用特定于越狱的攻击数据，但能够稳健地推广到多种越狱场景。学习到的更新随后被折叠到原始权重中，不会增加推理时的开销。在14个开放权重的语言和多模态模型上评估，NeST的表现优于轻量级基线，并以显著更少的可训练参数接近完全调优的鲁棒性。在仅文本模型上，NeST将平均越狱攻击成功率从44.5%降低到1.1%，同时平均仅训练0.4M参数。在多模态设置中，它将ASR从55.3%降低到1.1%，对于下游微调变体，它通过将ASR从53.8%降低到0.8%来恢复安全。这些结果表明，通过集中适应于局部、功能一致的安全结构，可以实现稳健、可维护的安全对齐。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for safety alignment in the deployment of Large Language Models (LLMs), highlighting the limitations of existing methods such as full fine-tuning, which is resource-intensive, and parameter-efficient techniques like Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA), which compromise safety consistency. The proposed NeST (Neuron-Selective Tuning) framework offers a novel approach by selectively tuning safety-relevant neurons based on activation patterns from harmful and benign prompts, allowing for efficient post-hoc safety alignment without modifying the entire model. This method is well-motivated as it aims to enhance safety while minimizing computational overhead. NeST demonstrates significant contributions by achieving robust safety alignment with fewer trainable parameters, outperforming lightweight baselines and approaching the effectiveness of full fine-tuning. In experiments, NeST reduced the average success rate of jailbreak attacks from 44.5% to 1.1% on text-only models, and similarly improved performance across multimodal settings, indicating that it effectively meets its safety alignment goals.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLM）部署中安全对齐的关键需求，强调了现有方法的局限性，如全量微调资源密集，以及像低秩适应（LoRA）这样的参数高效技术，这些方法在安全一致性上妥协。提出的神经元选择性调优（NeST）框架通过选择性调优与安全相关的神经元，利用激活探测识别这些神经元，同时保持模型的其他部分不变，提供了一种新颖的方法。该方法的动机在于实现高效的安全对齐，而无需传统微调的开销。NeST通过将文本模型的平均越狱攻击成功率从44.5%降低到1.1%，以及将多模态模型的成功率从55.3%降低到1.1%，同时仅训练极少量参数，显示了其在提高模型安全性方面的有效性。
HarmProfile: Characterizing Harmful Distributions in Frontier LLMs
Authors: Zhouyuan Ma, Yutao Wu, Hanxun Huang, Xiang Zheng, Xiao Liu, Yixin Cao, Zuxuan Wu, Xingjun Ma, Yu-Gang Jiang
First: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-11T09:39:32+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
Frontier large language models (LLMs) safety evaluation has largely treated harmful generation as an attack outcome rather than as an object of analysis. Consequently, little is known about the harmful outputs produced during model misbehavior, partly because large-scale, high-quality collections of frontier-LLM misbehavior are difficult to obtain. To address this gap, we introduce HarmProfile, a content-centric benchmark dataset that collects model misbehavior across diverse harm categories and model families, and defines the resulting harmful-output distribution as a model-level risk profile. The premise is that, just as linguistic behavior can be characterized from an utterance corpus, model risk can be characterized from the content, severity, and variation of its safety failures. HarmProfile contains over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs across 13 model families, organized into 15 harm categories and 57 subcategories. Using this corpus, we find that frontier LLMs reliably produce harmful content at scale, yet exhibit distinct risk profiles; both harmfulness and diversity grow with model capability, suggesting that frontier LLMs may appear safe yet harbor increasingly dangerous knowledge beneath the alignment surface. Our source code is available at https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile .
中文标题/摘要
标题：HarmProfile：前沿大型语言模型中有害分布的特征化
前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全评估在很大程度上将有害生成视为攻击结果，而非分析对象。因此，对于模型失常期间产生的有害输出知之甚少，部分原因是难以获得大规模、高质量的前沿LLM失常数据集。为了解决这一问题，我们引入了HarmProfile，这是一个以内容为中心的基准数据集，收集了来自不同有害类别和模型家族的模型失常，并将结果有害输出分布定义为模型级风险概况。前提是，正如语言行为可以从发言语料库中表征，模型风险可以从其安全失效的内容、严重性和变化中表征。HarmProfile包含来自13个模型家族的23个前沿LLM的超过80,000个经过验证的文档，组织成15个有害类别和57个子类别。利用该语料库，我们发现前沿LLM在规模上可靠地产生有害内容，但表现出不同的风险特征；有害性和多样性随着模型能力的提高而增长，这表明前沿LLM可能看似安全，但在对齐表面下潜藏着越来越危险的知识。我们的源代码可在https://github.com/fresh-ma/HarmProfile获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the lack of comprehensive analysis regarding harmful outputs generated by frontier large language models (LLMs), which have primarily been evaluated through the lens of attack outcomes rather than as a subject of study. Previous methods have struggled with the collection of large-scale, high-quality data on model misbehavior, leading to insufficient understanding of harmful distributions. The proposed approach, HarmProfile, offers a content-centric benchmark dataset that categorizes model misbehavior across various harm categories and model families, thereby enabling a detailed characterization of harmful-output distributions as model-level risk profiles. This methodology is well-motivated as it allows for the systematic analysis of safety failures based on the content, severity, and variation of harmful outputs. The study reveals that frontier LLMs consistently produce harmful content, with findings indicating that both harmfulness and diversity increase with model capability, highlighting the potential risks associated with seemingly safe models. The dataset comprises over 80,000 validated artifacts from 23 frontier LLMs, organized into 15 harm categories, providing a substantial resource for further research in model safety evaluation.
本研究解决了关于前沿大型语言模型（LLMs）生成的有害输出缺乏全面分析的问题，这些输出主要被视为攻击结果而非研究对象。以往的方法在记录模型不当行为的高质量数据集方面存在困难，导致对有害生成的理解不足。所提出的方法HarmProfile引入了一个以内容为中心的基准数据集，分类了不同类型和模型家族的模型不当行为，有效地基于安全失败的内容和严重性创建了模型级风险概况。这一方法论具有良好的动机，因为它允许对有害输出进行系统评估，揭示前沿LLMs持续产生有害内容，同时表现出与模型能力相关的独特风险特征。研究结果表明，输出的有害性和多样性随着模型能力的提高而增加，这表明即使看似安全的模型也可能存在危险知识，从而为LLMs的安全评估提供了有价值的见解。
Geometry-Guided Constraint Learning for LLM Safety Classification
Authors: Fumiaki Uehara, Koo Imai, Masato Tsutsumi, Keigo Kansa, Sora Usui, Yuki Kobiyama
First: 2026-06-09T08:47:00+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-09T08:47:00+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety as Polytope (SaP) learns linear half-space constraints in LLM hidden space but requires per-category tuning of the constraint count K. We show that sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature extraction resolves this: K=2 becomes optimal for 12/14 categories on Qwen3.5-9B, achieving 96-99% accuracy per category on our BeaverTails classification benchmark, largely eliminating the need for exhaustive sweeps (K=4-25 with random initialization). This convergence to two planes is consistent with the Linear Representation Hypothesis, providing suggestive evidence that safety boundaries in this setting admit a low-dimensional linear description in the SAE feature space. Building on this geometric perspective, we introduce a cone constraint whose learnable aperture adapts to each category's cluster concentration, stabilized by a three-phase training
中文标题/摘要
标题：几何引导的约束学习用于大型语言模型安全分类
安全作为多面体（SaP）在大型语言模型隐藏空间中学习线性半空间约束，但需要对每个类别调整约束数量K。我们表明，稀疏自编码器（SAE）特征提取解决了这个问题：在Qwen3.5-9B上，K=2对12/14个类别是最优的，在我们的BeaverTails分类基准上每个类别的准确率达到96-99%，大大减少了对全面搜索（K=4-25，随机初始化）的需求。这种收敛到两个平面与线性表示假设一致，提供了暗示性证据，表明在这种设置下的安全边界在SAE特征空间中允许低维线性描述。在这一几何视角的基础上，我们引入了一种锥约束，其可学习的开口适应每个类别的聚类浓度，通过三阶段训练进行稳定。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of safety classification in large language models (LLMs), specifically focusing on the limitations of the existing Safety as Polytope (SaP) method, which requires extensive tuning of the constraint count for different categories. The proposed approach utilizes sparse autoencoder (SAE) feature extraction to optimize the constraint count, demonstrating that a fixed count of K=2 is sufficient for 12 out of 14 categories, significantly improving accuracy to between 96-99% on the BeaverTails classification benchmark while reducing the need for exhaustive parameter sweeps. This method is well-motivated by the Linear Representation Hypothesis, suggesting that safety boundaries can be effectively described in a low-dimensional space. The paper contributes a novel cone constraint that adapts to the concentration of data clusters, supported by a three-phase training process, ultimately achieving enhanced performance in safety classification tasks.
本研究解决了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全分类的挑战，特别关注现有的安全多面体（SaP）方法的局限性，该方法需要针对不同类别进行广泛的约束计数调优。提出的方法利用稀疏自编码器（SAE）特征提取来优化约束计数，证明对于14个类别中的12个，固定的K=2计数足以实现96-99%的高准确率，基于BeaverTails分类基准。这种方法不仅简化了调优过程，还与线性表示假设一致，表明安全边界可以在低维空间中描述。本文的贡献在于引入了一种具有可学习开口的锥约束，能够适应特定类别的聚类浓度，通过三阶段训练方法进行验证，最终支持了在LLMs中高效且准确的安全分类目标。
PreAct-Bench: Benchmarking Predictive Monitoring in LLMs
Authors: Hainiu Xu, Italo Luis da Silva, Jiangnan Ye, Yuhao Wang, Wei Liu, Linyi Yang, Jonathan Richard Schwarz, Nicola Paoletti, Yulan He, Hanqi Yan
First: 2026-06-03T13:26:41+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-03T13:26:41+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed as autonomous agents capable of executing multi-step action trajectories toward a given objective. While existing safety research has focused on detecting unethical behavior from complete trajectories, this paradigm is fundamentally retrospective: it identifies harm only after it has already occurred. In this work, we study a critical yet overlooked safety task, which we term Predictive Monitoring: given only a partial action trajectory, can a model infer whether it will culminate in an unethical action before the overt action is executed? To support this task, we present PreActBench, a benchmark of 1,000 paired ethical and unethical action trajectories spanning five domains. We evaluate a range of LLMs, safety guardrail models, and latent probing methods across varying fractions of the action trajectory using our Prefix Foresight F1 metric. Results show that while humans achieve promising performance, predictive monitoring remains challenging even for strong models, highlighting the need for future-oriented risk reasoning in LLM safety.
中文标题/摘要
标题：PreAct-Bench：大语言模型预测监控基准测试
大型语言模型（LLMs）越来越多地被部署为能够执行多步骤行动轨迹以实现特定目标的自主代理。虽然现有的安全研究集中在检测完整轨迹中的不道德行为，但这种范式在根本上是回顾性的：它仅在伤害发生后才进行识别。在本研究中，我们研究了一个关键但被忽视的安全任务，我们称之为预测监控：仅给定部分行动轨迹，模型能否推断出它是否会在显性行动执行之前以不道德的行动告终？为支持这一任务，我们提出了PreAct-Bench，这是一个涵盖五个领域的1,000对伦理和不伦理行动轨迹的基准测试。我们使用我们的Prefix Foresight F1指标评估了一系列LLMs、安全护栏模型和潜在探测方法，涵盖不同的行动轨迹比例。结果表明，尽管人类表现出良好的性能，但即使对于强大的模型，预测监控仍然具有挑战性，突显了LLM安全中未来导向风险推理的必要性。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the growing deployment of large language models (LLMs) as autonomous agents and highlights the limitations of existing safety measures that only assess unethical behavior after it occurs. Previous methods have focused on complete action trajectories, which are retrospective and fail to prevent harm proactively. This paper introduces a novel approach called Predictive Monitoring, which aims to determine the likelihood of unethical actions based on partial action trajectories. The authors present PreAct-Bench, a benchmark consisting of 1,000 ethical and unethical action trajectory pairs across five domains, and evaluate various LLMs and safety models using the Prefix Foresight F1 metric. The findings indicate that while human performance is promising, predictive monitoring remains a significant challenge for even advanced models, underscoring the necessity for proactive risk assessment in LLM safety.
本研究关注大型语言模型（LLMs）作为自主代理的日益广泛应用，并强调现有安全措施在事后检测不道德行为方面的局限性。以往的方法主要集中在完整的行动轨迹上，这无法进行主动的风险评估。本文提出了一种新方法，称为预测监控，旨在根据部分行动轨迹判断潜在的不道德行为。作者提出了PreAct-Bench，这是一个包含1000对跨五个领域的伦理和不伦理行动轨迹的基准，并使用新的指标Prefix Foresight F1评估各种LLMs和安全模型。研究结果表明，尽管人类表现良好，但即使是先进模型的预测监控仍然是一个重大挑战，强调了在LLM安全中进行面向未来的风险推理的必要性。
TamperBench: Systematically Stress-Testing LLM Safety Under Fine-Tuning and Tampering
Authors: Saad Hossain, Tom Tseng, Punya Syon Pandey, Samanvay Vajpayee, Matthew Kowal, Nayeema Nonta, Samuel Simko, Stephen Casper, Zhijing Jin, Kellin Pelrine, Sirisha Rambhatla
First: 2026-02-06T18:04:38+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-02T23:27:30+00:00
Comments: 25 pages, 15 figures
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2 · Code3
Abstract
As increasingly capable open-weight large language models (LLMs) are deployed, improving their tamper resistance against unsafe modifications, whether accidental or intentional, becomes critical to minimize risks. However, there is no standard approach to evaluate tamper resistance. Varied datasets, metrics, and tampering configurations make it difficult to compare safety, utility, and robustness across different models and defenses. To address this, we introduce TamperBench, the first unified framework to systematically evaluate the tamper resistance of LLMs. TamperBench (i) curates a repository of state-of-the-art weight-space fine-tuning attacks, latent-space representation attacks, and alignment-stage defenses; (ii) enables realistic adversarial evaluation through systematic hyperparameter sweeps per attack-model pair; and (iii) provides both safety and utility evaluations. We use TamperBench to evaluate 21 open-weight LLMs, including defense-augmented variants, across nine tampering threats using standardized safety and capability metrics with hyperparameter sweeps per model-attack pair. The results provide insights including effects of post-training on tamper resistance, that jailbreak-tuning is typically the most severe attack, and that current alignment-stage defenses largely fail to withstand attack sweeps. Code is available at https://github.com/criticalml-uw/TamperBench.
中文标题/摘要
标题：TamperBench：系统性压力测试LLM在微调和篡改下的安全性
随着越来越强大的开放权重大型语言模型（LLM）的部署，提高其对意外或故意不安全修改的抗篡改能力变得至关重要，以最小化风险。然而，目前没有标准的方法来评估抗篡改能力。不同的数据集、指标和篡改配置使得比较不同模型和防御的安全性、实用性和鲁棒性变得困难。为了解决这个问题，我们引入了TamperBench，这是第一个统一框架，用于系统性评估LLM的抗篡改能力。TamperBench (i) 汇集了最先进的权重空间微调攻击、潜在空间表示攻击和对齐阶段防御的库；(ii) 通过对每个攻击-模型对进行系统的超参数搜索，实现现实的对抗性评估；(iii) 提供安全性和实用性评估。我们使用TamperBench评估了21个开放权重LLM，包括增强防御的变体，针对九种篡改威胁使用标准化的安全性和能力指标，并对每个模型-攻击对进行超参数搜索。结果提供了包括后训练对抗篡改能力的影响、越狱微调通常是最严重的攻击，以及当前对齐阶段防御在抵御攻击时大多失败的见解。代码可在https://github.com/criticalml-uw/TamperBench获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the critical need for improved tamper resistance in large language models (LLMs) as their deployment increases, highlighting the lack of standardized evaluation methods for assessing tamper resistance. Previous methods have been inconsistent due to varied datasets, metrics, and configurations, making it challenging to compare the safety and robustness of different models. The proposed TamperBench framework offers a unified approach to systematically evaluate LLM tamper resistance by curating a repository of attacks and defenses, enabling realistic adversarial evaluations, and providing comprehensive safety and utility assessments. This paper contributes by evaluating 21 open-weight LLMs against nine tampering threats, revealing significant insights such as the severe impact of jailbreak-tuning and the inadequacy of current defenses. The methodology involves systematic hyperparameter sweeps for each model-attack pair, resulting in standardized performance metrics that support the goal of enhancing LLM safety.

When Autoregressive Consistency Hurts Safety Alignment
Authors: Bochen Lyu, Yiyang Jia, Xiaohao Cai, Zhanxing Zhu
First: 2026-06-02T19:32:23+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-02T19:32:23+00:00
Comments: 21 pages
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Safety alignment in large language models (LLMs) is fragile in part because it is often shallow: fine-tuning mainly reshapes the model's behavior near the first few output tokens. We argue that this phenomenon can be understood through autoregressive consistency, the tendency of next-token prediction to preserve and extend the current response trajectory consistently. By analyzing the learning dynamics of safety alignment, we show that autoregressive consistency can concentrate alignment updates on early tokens, offering a mechanistic explanation for shallow safety alignment. The same mechanism also predicts a broader class of attacks on LLMs: attacks that induce harmful continuation states at arbitrary positions in the output trajectory. As a concrete example, we introduce random insertion attack, which inserts a short harmful span into an otherwise safe refusal trajectory and exploits autoregressive consistency to sustain the resulting harmful branch, thereby bypassing safety alignment. Notably, a short harmful span can redirect the generation to be harmful even after a long refusal prefix, highlighting autoregressive consistency as a potential broader failure mechanism. This suggests that safety alignment should also break harmful autoregressive consistency throughout the output trajectory. We therefore propose adversarial safety alignment, an initial framework based on worst-case harmful continuation states, and instantiate it with random worst-insertion training. Overall, our results suggest that autoregressive consistency should be treated as a central consideration in both safety alignment and attack design.
中文标题/摘要
标题：自回归一致性如何影响安全对齐
大型语言模型（LLMs）的安全对齐脆弱，部分原因在于其通常较浅：微调主要重塑模型在前几个输出标记附近的行为。我们认为这一现象可以通过自回归一致性来理解，即下一个标记预测保持并延续当前响应轨迹的一致性倾向。通过分析安全对齐的学习动态，我们表明自回归一致性可以将对齐更新集中在早期标记上，从而为浅层安全对齐提供了机制性解释。同样的机制也预测了一类更广泛的对LLMs的攻击：在输出轨迹的任意位置诱导有害延续状态的攻击。作为具体例子，我们引入随机插入攻击，该攻击将一个短的有害片段插入到原本安全的拒绝轨迹中，并利用自回归一致性来维持由此产生的有害分支，从而绕过安全对齐。值得注意的是，一个短的有害片段可以在长拒绝前缀后重新引导生成变得有害，突显自回归一致性作为潜在的更广泛失败机制。这表明安全对齐也应打破输出轨迹中有害的自回归一致性。因此，我们提出了对抗性安全对齐，这是一个基于最坏情况有害延续状态的初步框架，并通过随机最坏插入训练进行实例化。总体而言，我们的结果表明，自回归一致性应被视为安全对齐和攻击设计中的核心考虑。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the fragility of safety alignment in large language models (LLMs), which is often shallow and primarily focused on the initial output tokens. Previous methods have relied on fine-tuning that does not adequately address the tendency of autoregressive consistency to concentrate alignment updates on early tokens, leading to vulnerabilities. The proposed approach, adversarial safety alignment, aims to mitigate these issues by considering harmful continuation states throughout the output trajectory, rather than just at the beginning. This methodology includes random worst-insertion training to better handle potential attacks, such as the random insertion attack, which can exploit autoregressive consistency to create harmful outputs even after safe refusals. The findings indicate that addressing autoregressive consistency is crucial for improving safety alignment and preventing attacks, thereby contributing a novel framework to enhance the robustness of LLMs.
本研究探讨了大型语言模型（LLMs）中安全对齐的脆弱性，这种脆弱性往往是表面的，因为微调主要影响初始输出标记。以往的方法关注于对齐模型行为，但未考虑自回归一致性的影响，这可能导致表浅的安全对齐和易受攻击的脆弱性。所提出的方法，即对抗性安全对齐，旨在通过关注最坏情况下的有害延续状态并采用随机最坏插入训练来缓解这些问题。这种方法强调需要在整个输出轨迹中破坏有害的自回归一致性。研究结果表明，所提出的方法有效增强了安全对齐，通过展示对攻击的改善抵御能力，从而支持创建更安全的LLMs的目标。
Black-box, Adaptive, Efficient, Transferable, Harmful, Applicable... Attacks Are All You Need to Break LLMs
Authors: Vincent Limbach, Jonas Dornbusch, David Lüdke, Stephan Günnemann, Leo Schwinn
First: 2026-06-02T13:39:15+00:00 · Latest: 2026-06-02T13:39:15+00:00
Abs · PDF · Code1 · Code2
Abstract
Accurately evaluating adversarial robustness is a longstanding challenge. A flawed attack design can inflate robustness estimates, making deployment risk assessment and defense comparison unreliable. Historically, standardized attacks such as AutoAttack have largely resolved this for image classifiers, providing a reliable evaluation baseline for systematic comparison across defenses. However, no equivalent exists for LLM jailbreak evaluation yet, where designing such an attack is considerably more difficult. A reliable attack must, among other things, be black-box compatible, applicable to arbitrary defense pipelines, and efficient, which no existing method jointly satisfies. We introduce Indirect Harm Optimization (IHO), a masked diffusion language model attacker trained via iterative preference optimization against a harmfulness judge, requiring only black-box access to the target. The same method can be used without modification as a strong adaptive attack on individual behaviors, or as an efficient amortized policy that transfers to held-out behaviors and unseen target models without fine-tuning. Even against layered defenses, such as a Circuit Breaker-trained model combined with an auxiliary detector, IHO improves attack success considerably over state-of-the-art approaches, without any defense-specific adaptation. Our results position IHO as a practical step toward the kind of standardized jailbreak evaluation that has improved reliability in the past. Code and models are available on GitHub and Hugging Face.
中文标题/摘要
标题：黑箱、自适应、高效、可转移、有害、适用……攻击是破解LLM所需的一切
准确评估对抗鲁棒性一直是一个长期挑战。设计不当的攻击可能会夸大鲁棒性估计，使得部署风险评估和防御比较不可靠。历史上，标准化攻击如AutoAttack在图像分类器中基本解决了这一问题，为系统比较提供了可靠的评估基准。然而，目前尚无等效的LLM越狱评估标准，设计这样的攻击要困难得多。一个可靠的攻击必须在许多方面兼容黑箱，适用于任意防御管道，并且高效，而现有方法没有一个能够共同满足这些要求。我们引入了间接有害优化（IHO），这是一种通过迭代偏好优化训练的掩蔽扩散语言模型攻击者，仅需对目标进行黑箱访问。相同的方法可以在不修改的情况下作为对个体行为的强自适应攻击，或作为一种高效的摊销策略，能够转移到保留行为和未见目标模型上，而无需微调。即使面对分层防御，如与辅助检测器结合的电路断路器训练模型，IHO在攻击成功率上也显著优于最先进的方法，而无需任何特定于防御的适应。我们的结果将IHO定位为朝着标准化越狱评估迈出的实用一步，这在过去提高了可靠性。代码和模型可在GitHub和Hugging Face上获取。
Summary / 总结
The research addresses the challenge of accurately evaluating adversarial robustness in large language models (LLMs), highlighting that existing standardized attack methods for image classifiers do not translate effectively to LLMs, leading to unreliable robustness assessments. The proposed method, Indirect Harm Optimization (IHO), differs from past approaches by being a black-box compatible attacker that requires only black-box access to the target and can adapt to various defense mechanisms without modification. This method is well-motivated as it aims to provide a reliable evaluation baseline for LLM jailbreaks, which has been lacking. IHO employs a masked diffusion language model trained through iterative preference optimization against a harmfulness judge, demonstrating significant improvements in attack success rates against layered defenses compared to state-of-the-art methods. The findings suggest that IHO contributes to establishing a standardized framework for evaluating LLM vulnerabilities, thereby enhancing the reliability of robustness assessments.
本研究解决了准确评估大型语言模型（LLMs）对抗鲁棒性的问题，强调了现有攻击设计的不足，这些设计会夸大鲁棒性估计，妨碍可靠的风险评估。以往的方法，如针对图像分类器的标准化攻击，无法有效转化为LLMs，因为设计有效攻击的复杂性使得其难以实现黑箱兼容和适用于各种防御机制。所提出的间接危害优化（IHO）方法通过利用一个掩蔽扩散语言模型攻击者来克服这些局限性，该攻击者仅需黑箱访问，并且可以适应个体行为或在不进行微调的情况下转移到未见模型。论文表明，IHO显著提高了对分层防御的攻击成功率，确立了其作为LLMs标准化越狱评估的有前景的方法，从而有助于更可靠的对抗鲁棒性评估。
